- Changed: pg_sift: the parallel top-N scan (workers on `ORDER BY key LIMIT n`) bounds the superblocks of its walk by the zones of the sort keys after the first too (`sift.topn_zone_keys`), as the serial walk does: each participant tests the superblock it takes against the n-th row of its own heap on the keys in order, and the shared first-key rank as before, where a first key that ties between units kept every unit. 2 workers, 25,000 to 200,000 rows, 1,280 rows a unit, LIMIT 10: `ORDER BY k_mix, id` reads 16, 34, 71, 143 units without it and 5, 5, 5, 5 with it; `ORDER BY g DESC, id DESC` 20, 40, 79, 157 and 1, 2, 2, 2 (the 7 to 12 ms of the scan are the start of the workers at these sizes: the serial walk takes 0.1 to 1 ms). The rows are those of the plain plan with 0, 2 and 4 workers (`col_topn_parallel`).
- Added: pg_sift: a top-N over an inner join to a table that only filters (`SELECT f.. FROM fact f JOIN dim d ON d.k = f.k WHERE d.attr = x ORDER BY f.key LIMIT n`) runs in the column scan: the keys of the table's rows that pass its conditions become a set of the scan (`k IN (keys)`, the join filter the aggregate scans already use), which the top-N holds exactly. Taken only when the table has a unique index on its join column (no row matches twice), the join is inner on one integer column of the scanned table, the ON clause and WHERE read the table's columns alone, and the result has none of its columns; every other join keeps the normal plan (`sift.explain_refusals`: `topn_shape`). The set is read when the scan starts, so changes of the table between two runs count; no workers with a join. Normal plan / top-N join, 25,000 to 200,000 rows, `d.label < 'label_5'` (half the keys), LIMIT 10: 9.3, 18.7, 39.5, 88.1 ms / 0.30, 0.26, 0.39, 0.48 ms; `d.label = 'label_7'` (one key): 13.8, 16.7, 36.1, 71.4 ms / 0.28, 0.32, 0.95, 2.05 ms. Test `col_topn_join`: 17 shapes (7 taken, 10 refused) with the rows of the plain plan, a 120-query differential fuzz through changes of the table, deletes, moved rows, the write buffer and its promotion, and a generic plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: the outer aggregate over per-row aggregates (the arms of a `UNION ALL` grouped by a row id: counts, `sum` / `avg` of the cells, `count(DISTINCT id)` and `count(DISTINCT parent_id)`) runs with workers: a row is in one item of work, an item flushes the cells of its last row before its table is dumped, the leader adds the tables and gives the distinct ids of the key the arms group by its own key ids, and the float sums of the cells use the lanes (the rows are the same for 0, 2 and 4 workers and the normal plan's; the largest input of an inner `avg(float8)` travels with the tables). A lateral `LIMIT 1` child of a unique key inside the arms is shared with the workers as a lookup table. The planner prices the workers on the arms' work (about 35 to 70 ns a tuple an arm) and launches none below the break-even: 200,000 documents of 2.3 elements, two arms with a parent id and a float average: 64.6 / 47.9 / 25.0 ms with 0 / 2 / 4 workers, the same with no hidden key 32.9 / 32.0 / 11.0, three joins (a `VALUES` list of spans, a table, a unique-key lookup) 56.1 / 31.0 / 25.0; at 25,000 and 50,000 documents the natural plan stays on one process (workers cost 1.5 to 2 ms there) and from 100,000 it takes the workers PostgreSQL gives the table (1 at 100,000, 2 at 200,000; `ALTER TABLE .. SET (parallel_workers = n)` raises them). Sums over a joined column (`SAA_M_*`), a hashed `NOT IN` sub-plan and a child plan run per combination of leaves keep one process (their scans take 7, 4 and 61 ms for 200,000 documents).
- Added: pg_sift: `test/t/pg_upgrade.sh` runs `pg_upgrade --check` and the upgrade of a cluster with `sift`, `sift_col` and `sift_table` objects (a tablespace, enum, uuid, interval and timestamptz leaves, JSON paths, open write buffers and promoted segments) from the previous PostgreSQL major to this one when both are installed (PostgreSQL 17 to 18 verified; `SIFT_PG_OLD_BIN` names another), otherwise to the same major, and checks the answers, `sift.verify`, `sift.col_verify`, the preflight, and writes, `VACUUM` and merges afterwards. Nothing in the files or the library needed a change. `docs/UPGRADING.md` has the commands (preflight, `ALTER EXTENSION UPDATE`, `pg_upgrade`, going back to an older release).
- Added: pg_sift: an upgrade preflight, `scripts/upgrade-check.sh [--to RELEASE] [--strict] DATABASE...` (`--all` for every database), which runs `sift.upgrade_check()` (`scripts/upgrade_check.sql`, loaded in a transaction that is rolled back, so a database whose extension is older can be checked) and prints one row per finding with its status (`ok`, `warn`, `blocker`) and advice: the extension against the library, the metapage format and column formats of every `sift` index, `sift_col` index and `sift_table` and the first release that reads them (`--to 3.1` makes what 3.1 cannot read a blocker, with the `sift.col_format_minor` and the `REINDEX` or rewrite that write what it reads), options that wait for a `REINDEX` (`cube_minmax` and JSON `paths` on older units, the deprecated `mutable_limit`), settings in the server, `ALTER DATABASE` and `ALTER ROLE` that the library does not define, that switch a feature off or that keep an older column format, and test-only objects left behind. `sift.index_format(rel)` is the one new library function (the metapage format of a relation). No format change. Test `upgrade_check`.
- Added: pg_sift: `superunit` (`sift_col` reloption, 0 = off, the default, or 2 to 256): the merged sums and cube of every group of that many consecutive units of a segment are stored once more (0.5% to 1.5% of the index), and a scan whose units are all exact answers a group from that one summary: `GROUP BY` a 20-value key with `count` and `sum` over 400,000 rows (391 units, groups of 64): 3.09 ms from the units, 0.22 ms from the groups, and 0.18 to 0.22 ms from 25,000 to 400,000 rows where the units go from 0.34 to 3.09 ms. A group with a page written since the last VACUUM, a predicate that cuts through it or a shape its summary lacks (a float sum) is read as units: the rows are the same (`sift.agg_super = off` is the units' answer). Column format minor 12, written only for an index with the option under `sift.col_format_minor = 12` (the default; 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10 keep the formats their libraries read): an index without the option writes the bytes it wrote before. A 3.2 library refuses a minor 8 segment with the REINDEX hint. Units written before the option have no summary; `REINDEX` or a merge writes them.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a query that casts the member of a `jsonb_each` to two types (`sum(CAST(e.value AS integer)), avg(CAST(e.value AS float8))`) next to a value the leaf cannot store raised the message of the float8 cast where the normal plan raises the one of the cast it evaluates first (`integer`); such a query keeps the normal plan. Several dirty rows with different errors raise the one each plan reaches first. Test `agg_member_cast_dirty`.
- Changed: pg_sift: `LEFT JOIN LATERAL jsonb_each(doc) r ON r.key = 'k'` (the member of one key) runs in SiftAgg on PostgreSQL 17 too, as it does on 18, while no unit may hold a JSON null there: the cast of a JSON null raises before 18 where the scan reads NULL, so a unit with one keeps the normal plan (the same guard as the casts of a path). No format change. Tests `agg_left_each`, `agg_dirty_semantics`, `agg_correlated_fanout` (their PG17 expected file is gone).
- Changed: pg_sift: a comparison, `IN` list or `IS NULL` test of the cast of a `jsonb_each` member (`WHERE CAST(e.value AS float8) > 3`) next to an aggregate that casts it no longer keeps the normal plan when some unit holds a value the leaf cannot store: the row read again raises the cast error before the test, as the normal plan does for every member that reaches it. Another test of the members, a join or a conjunct that may raise in the same query keeps the normal plan. No format change. Test `agg_member_cast_dirty`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: the first write of a `sift_table` writes the metapage and then the directory block; a session that looked at the file in between found one block and failed reading the second (a read error, no damage), and a second first writer skipped the layout for the same reason. A file shorter than both blocks is a table not laid out yet. Test `009_tbl_concurrent` (the state is made by cutting the file after a clean stop).
- Changed: pg_sift: a group key that is an `int8` or a `numeric(p,s)` column (not an expression of it) is answered from the cube's histogram of the column, as an `int4` or text key is, where the units were scanned: 200,000 rows, `GROUP BY` an int8 or a numeric key with `count`, `sum`: 1.9 to 2.2 ms scanned, 0.19 to 0.22 ms from the histogram.
- Added: pg_sift: `sift.table_async_promote` (off by default): the backend of a `sift_table` that closes a write buffer asks a background worker (the one of `sift.table_auto_defrag`, `sift.async_maintenance` on) to promote it, freeze the settled groups and merge, where it did that itself. Single-row autocommit `INSERT`s, 100,000 rows, one client, 4 MB buffers: the two statements that closed a buffer took 25-31 ms (about 55 ms in all) and now at most 2.8 ms; the median, p99 and p99.9 are unchanged. From half of the directory on the writer still settles itself. No format change. Test `018_tbl_async_promote` (a heap twin, a crash while the writers and the worker run, `sift.async_maintenance` off).
- Added: pg_sift: `sift.table_backpressure` (0 = off, the default, or a percent above 50): a writer of a `sift_table` that needs a new write buffer while the directory is past that percent waits for the maintenance lock and merges (up to 16 steps instead of 4) before it goes on, where it waited only from the last two entries. No format change. Isolation spec `tbl_backpressure` (a session holding the maintenance lock), `tbl_cap`.
- Changed: pg_sift: `sift_table` keeps up to four fetch scans, one per superblock an index scan or join fetches from, where it kept one that decoded a whole unit again each time the fetches moved to another superblock (an index scan over updated rows, whose versions sit in two): a self-insert scan over 16,000 rows of updated 1 KB rows takes 0.2 s where it took 9.9 s (PG18).
- Added: pg_sift: a `sift_table` stores, with the cube of every unit, the calendar cells of each `date`, `timestamp` and `timestamptz` column (column format minor 8, written under `sift.col_format_minor` 8 or more; a 3.2 library refuses such a segment, 7 or less writes none): the groups of its days, else of its months, else of its years, the finest that fits 1,024 cells of 8 rows or more and 5% of the unit's bytes, the grain recorded in each unit (a `timestamptz` is cut in UTC days). `date_trunc` (year to week and day), `extract` and `date_part` (year, quarter, month, week, isoyear, dow, isodow, doy, day), `::date` and their combinations as the `GROUP BY` key, a range on the column, and `extract(...) = k` or `IN` on a date are answered from the cells, without a stored generated column, where every row was read; a key that changes within a day (`hour`) or reads the session time zone of a `timestamptz` (write `AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`) scans, and says why in `sift.col_rollup_misses`. Opt out: `SET sift.col_format_minor = 7` before the writes. A `sift_col` index asks for the same cells with the reloption `calendar_columns = 'ts, d'` or `'all'` (default off, `ALTER INDEX ... SET`; only a schema without JSON paths). Details, grains and sizes: docs/COL-PREAGG.md section 2.
- Added: pg_sift: `rollup_dims` (`sift_col` reloption, default off, `rollup_dims = 'kind, sub'` or `'kind, sub: amount'`): every unit written afterwards also holds the rollup of its rows by two to six scalar columns, a cell for each distinct combination with its rows and, per integer or numeric measure, the count, the exact sum, the minimum and the maximum. `GROUP BY` of two or more of them, a test of one with a group by another, and a time bucket with a column are answered from the cells of the exact units (`Pre-aggregate Rollup: rows`, `Units From Pre-aggregates` in `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`), where the one-key cube scanned them: 200,000 rows, `GROUP BY kind, sub` 2.1 ms scanned, 0.63 ms from the cells; `GROUP BY month, kind` 3.2 ms, 0.41 ms; the index grows 0.9% to 4.6% for two low-cardinality columns and 19% for three. A unit keeps at most a tenth of its rows in cells (64 to 4,096): a column of 300 or more values stores nothing and the units scan, with the same answer. Column format minor 9, written only for an index with the option under `sift.col_format_minor = 9` (the default; 8 and lower ignore it and write what a 3.2, 3.1 and 3.0 library read): an index without the option writes the bytes it wrote before. A 3.2 library refuses a minor 9 segment with the REINDEX hint. Units written before the option scan; `REINDEX`, a merge or `sift.col_refresh` writes the cells.
- Added: pg_sift: `cube_minmax` (`sift_col` reloption, default off): the cube of every unit written afterwards also holds the minimum and maximum of each integer measure per group, and `min()`/`max()` of a measure grouped by a dim of the cube are answered from the groups of the exact units (200,000 rows: 2.6 ms scanned, 0.23 ms from the groups; the index grows 1.0% to 3.0%). Column format minor 7, written only for an index with the option under `sift.col_format_minor = 7` (the default; 3, 4 and 6 keep the formats a 3.2, 3.1 and 3.0 library reads): an index without the option writes the bytes it wrote before. A 3.2 library refuses a minor 7 segment with the REINDEX hint. Units written before the option scan; `REINDEX` or a merge writes them.
- Added: pg_sift: the arms of a two-level aggregate (`UNION ALL` of per-row aggregates under an outer aggregate) can be joined to a spine of buckets, a `VALUES` list, a series, a small table or a sub-select, with `spine JOIN arms ON m.key = s.key` or `spine LEFT JOIN arms ON ..` (also `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`, or `jsonb_build_array(..) = jsonb_build_array(..)` on several columns): `Custom Scan (SiftAgg)` reads the spine's rows once, adds each cell of the scan to the group of every spine row its keys equal, and the spine's columns are group keys; with `LEFT JOIN` a spine row no cell matched is a group of NULL arm columns with `count(*)` 1, so empty buckets stay. The normal plan joined every per-row group with the spine before grouping: 200,000 documents with a 3-key lookup in the arms and a two-row spine, 23,004 ms in the normal plan and 23,391 ms with the arms scanned alone, 205 ms as one scan (25,000: 1,114 / 1,121 / 40 ms; 50,000: 1,757 / 1,736 / 68; 100,000: 3,172 / 3,150 / 105; median of 3, one backend, busy host, scan chosen with `seq_page_cost = random_page_cost = 1000`). Any other join, a condition that is no equality, a WHERE that reads the arms and a spine of more than 10,000 rows keep the normal plan. Test `agg_two_level_spine`.
- Changed: pg_sift: documented and tested: a `LIMIT 1` child over a key the table repeats, without an `ORDER BY` that makes the order total, keeps the child, since the row it returns is the first its plan meets and the index and the heap order differ for a row updated in place (`agg_first_row_lookup`).
- Changed: pg_sift: the planner counts the values a column of a correlated child can take as the child's rows for each distinct input of its own lookup, not as one: a second `LEFT JOIN LATERAL (.. LIMIT 1)` keyed by a column of the first's row was priced for a single input and kept its child, which then ran for every distinct value. Both laterals are now lookup tables when their tables are small against their inputs (two keys then a text key, 60,000 facts with 60,000 distinct inputs, median of 3, warm, 2026-10-22, PostgreSQL 18.6: tables of 25k / 50k / 100k / 200k rows 19.7 / 34.9 / 44.6 / 33.6 ms, were 28.0 / 60.7 / 79.8 / 72.6 ms with the second child run per input; 72 / 108 / 202 / 162 ms in the normal plan, host busy).
- Changed: pg_sift: a `LIMIT 1` child with an `ORDER BY` (`.. WHERE t.a = x AND t.b = y ORDER BY t.ts DESC LIMIT 1`, the latest row of a key) is read as a first-row lookup when a unique index holds the key and the order columns, so that no two rows tie: up to three integer, date or timestamp columns, either direction, `NULLS FIRST` or `LAST`; an order column the index holds must be `NOT NULL` (a primary key in the order does it). The lookup table keeps the first row of each key by the order (among the rows that pass a condition on the row, as in the previous entry). A repeated version, a text or expression order, a nullable order column under a unique index and more than three columns keep the child. Two keys over three versions of each, 60,000 facts with 60,000 distinct inputs, median of 3, warm, 2026-10-22, PostgreSQL 18.6: tables of 25k / 50k / 100k / 200k rows 6.4 / 11.4 / 18.8 / 13.2 ms against 36.8 / 65.6 / 79.9 / 84.0 ms in the normal plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: a `LIMIT 1` child that tests its row besides the unique key (`.. WHERE t.a = x AND t.b = y AND t.score > 100 LIMIT 1`: comparisons of columns with constants, null tests, `AND`/`OR`/`NOT`) is read as a first-row lookup too: the lookup table is loaded with the rows that pass, and a key whose row fails has none, as the child returns none. A condition that can raise an error, a list, a function or the outer row's value keeps the child. Three keys and a condition, 60,000 facts with 60,000 distinct inputs (median of 3, warm, 2026-10-22, PostgreSQL 18.6): table of 25k / 50k / 100k / 200k rows 12.6 / 20.5 / 27.4 / 52.2 ms against 52 / 111 / 145 / 191 ms in the normal plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: the planner prices a row of a table read as the first-row lookup of a `LIMIT 1` child at what it costs (0.2 microseconds to hash, against 1.6 for a run of the child), not as a row of any joined table: the lookup is taken when the table has fewer than about 8 rows for each distinct input, where it was 2. Three keys, 60,000 facts with 60,000 distinct inputs (median of 3, warm, 2026-10-22, PostgreSQL 18.6): a 200,000-row table 32.6 ms (143 ms in the normal plan, about 100 ms when the child ran in the scan), 100,000 rows 25 ms (105 ms), 50,000 rows 15 ms (75 ms), 25,000 rows 9 ms (45 ms). Few inputs keep the child.
- Changed: pg_sift: a `SiftAgg` path reports to the plan above the groups of every row the lookup lists of the scan can pass, where PostgreSQL's count of a list is a default guess (the rows of a sub-select or a CTE, a filter on a jsonb path): a CTE of one row no longer makes a join of the aggregate to a grouped copy of itself on a null-safe key run that copy's aggregate again for each row. The price of the path is not changed; the plan above it is now priced for those groups, so the scan is taken where that plan was a nested loop priced for one row (the neutral shape at 50k / 100k / 200k rows: groups estimated 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 against 16,667 / 33,333 / 66,667 made, where it was 2 / 5 / 10).
- Changed: pg_sift: the planner prices the opening of the partitions of a partitioned table, which grows faster than their number (the scan takes 0.75 ms more than the normal plan's `Append` at 200 partitions, 9 ms at 800, 27 ms at 1,600 and 111 ms at 3,200, 11 ns for each pair of partitions), where it priced the scan as if it opened one source. 200,000 rows in 100 / 400 / 1,200 / 3,200 partitions, `GROUP BY` of one column, planning plus execution (median of 5, warm, 2026-10-22, PostgreSQL 18.6, a busy host): 100 and 400 keep the scan (3.3 ms against 34.5 ms, 22.8 against 39.5), 1,200 now takes the normal plan (46 to 52 ms, where the scan took 53 to 57), 3,200 takes it too (106 to 148 ms, where the scan took 366 to 450). A plan that prunes to a few partitions at plan time pays for those only.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a filter on a joined table that sits in the `WHERE` of a sub-select on the nullable side of a `LEFT JOIN` from a spine (the periods of a report) filters the rows of the scan again before the match, where PostgreSQL's move of that `WHERE` into the join's `ON` clause made it a test of the spine's lookup: the joined table was read whole, and a filter that matches nothing no longer ended the scan, which read every unit (an events table joined to a dimension table whose filter matches no row, two periods: 25k / 100k / 200k rows 2.7 / 4.2 / 7.8 ms and 2 / 8 / 16 units, now 0.05 / 0.06 / 0.04 ms and none, as the normal plan's 0.04 ms; median of 3, warm, 2026-10-22, PostgreSQL 18.6). A condition that reads the spine stays in the match.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg reads a partitioned table also when a parameter of a generic plan or a stable function (`d >= $1`, `k = ANY ($1)`, `ts >= now() - interval '30 days'`) prunes its partitions when the plan starts: the scan evaluates the operand when it starts and does not read the partitions whose bounds it refutes (`Partitions Removed` in `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`; parallel workers take the leader's superblocks). Before, a parameter on the partition key kept the normal plan, and a stable bound read every partition. A table split by hash on a column compared with a parameter keeps the normal plan.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg joins a lateral sub-select, a `UNION` / `UNION ALL` of arms, `generate_series`, `unnest` of an array column or a set-returning function that reads the joined row. PostgreSQL's parameterized plan of it is the child, run once per distinct input, with duplicates, NULL-extended rows and errors as in the normal plan. A call of a document column that cannot raise (`jsonb_exists`, `->>`, `jsonb_typeof`) takes the index expression column of that call as the parameter. A `UNION ALL` / `UNION` of sub-selects, a sub-select of a statement-level CTE and a `WITH` inside the sub-select are lookup plans too.
- Added: pg_sift: the rows of a joined table can be kept in shared memory for the other backends (`sift.agg_lookup_shared_kb`, 0 = off by default, superuser; dynamic shared memory taken as entries are kept, no restart or preload): a backend that loads a table writes its rows once in the flat form of a parallel scan, and a backend that finds the table unchanged (the check of `sift.agg_lookup_cache_kb`: all pages visible, same LSNs) reads them in place, so a fresh connection pays the attach, not the load. First query of a fresh backend, 60,000-row fact table, one integer key, load / shared read: 25,000 mapping rows 11.1 / 9.8 ms, 50,000 13.1 / 10.4, 100,000 21.5 / 12.1, 200,000 35.6 / 17.0; three text keys 18.7 / 14.2, 24.0 / 16.4, 45.6 / 18.1, 52.0 / 25.9 (the attach itself is 0.6 ms at 200,000 rows); eight fresh connections at once, 200,000 rows: slowest 59.5 ms, 13.5 ms with the entry there. The backend that writes the entry pays for it once (200,000 rows: +16 ms). Tests `agg_lookup_shared`, isolation spec `agg_lookup_shared`, TAP test `018_lookup_shared` (a backend killed while it reads an entry).
- Fixed: `make check` on macOS. The stock bash 3.2 failed on the syntax of bash 4 and `make check` exited 0 with no test run; it now stops with `needs bash 4+ (brew install bash)`, a run that selects no job fails, and `scripts/cpu_time.py` and the job claim no longer need Linux (`prctl`, `flock`). `scripts/ci.sh --selftest` checks that a script with bash 4 constructs carries that first-line check. CI builds the Homebrew formula and runs `brew test` and a few regression tests on macOS arm64.
- Changed: with the directory of a `sift` or `sift_col` index full and the merges failing, a forced seal (`sift.promote()`, `sift.merge(index, true)`) leaves the open write buffer open with a NOTICE instead of failing with "has 128 segments", and the error of the refresh that finds no write buffer says what the last failed step was; the limit is `max_segments` of `sift.test_limits` in a test build. Test `dir_full` (isolation).
- Added: `sift.workers`, one row per `sift` and `sift_col` index with the segments, the sealed write buffers waiting, whether maintenance is running, and, when the last background step failed, the failures in a row, when, the earliest retry, the SQLSTATE and the message (the log was the only place; the metapage keeps the last one in space the page had: no format minor, builds without it ignore it and read pages without it as no failure).
- Changed: `sift.standby_conflicts` is on by default: the primary logs a recovery conflict when it reuses blocks a merge freed, so a hot standby without `hot_standby_feedback` waits for its long query up to `max_standby_streaming_delay` and then cancels it, as for a heap query, where it failed at once with "segment recycled during scan". `off` restores that. Test `maint_standby`.
- Changed: `sift.col_auto_merge` promotes the write buffer of a `sift_col` index and leaves its segments when the buffer is the only reason (an insert-only table no longer has its whole index rewritten at every 1% of new rows: 12 cycles of 2% inserts on 200,000 rows wrote 4.6 MB of segments, from 33.4 MB, with the same query times); new settings `sift.col_auto_merge_wal_pct` (the rewrite of all segments waits until the server has written 100 / that percent times the bytes of the index since the index was last written) and `sift.col_auto_merge_max_active` (it waits, with a backoff, while that many client backends run a statement); the churn simulator `bench/churn/churn.sh` measures them.
- Changed: when the filters of the source keep under a 32nd of a unit's rows (a scope of one to five keys that are spread over every unit), `SiftAgg` goes on with the unit as those rows alone, renumbered as in the unit of a match set, where the leaves, the offsets and owners of the elements of a list and the aggregates still worked over the whole unit. A one-key scope over 25k-200k rows took 0.17-0.40 ms (scalar columns) and 0.33-2.4 ms (the elements of a list) and now takes 0.11-0.33 and 0.24-1.3 ms, against 0.08-0.24 and 0.12-0.55 ms for the index probes of the normal plan; five keys at 200k rows 0.90 to 0.75 ms and 2.5 to 1.8 ms. Ten keys and more, and the units with rows read again from the heap, are unchanged. No on-disk change.
- Fixed: pg_sift: the first statement of a session that has not preloaded the library ran the ordinary plan when it had bound parameters (the extended protocol of a driver, `pgbench -M extended` or `-M prepared`, `EXECUTE` of a prepared statement with arguments): 220 to 260 ms against 3 to 5 ms for the next one, with no `Custom Scan (SiftAgg)` in the plan. The executor start plans it again through the hook with the parameters' types and values, as it did for a statement without parameters. A query inside a function keeps its plan. `agg_first_statement` covers both.
- Fixed: pg_sift: an average or sum over a `numeric` column of a precision past 18 digits (`numeric(30, 16)`) whose units hold many distinct values was planned as the scan, which is slower than the normal plan there (each unit builds an entry for each new value); the planner now prices an entry as measured and keeps the normal plan for such a column, and the scan for the column of few values. The answers are the same in both.
- Fixed: `sift_table`: an `UPDATE` or `DELETE` that scans a table while a `VACUUM` (or the background defrag) replaces its tombstone log failed with `failed to fetch tuple being updated`; the statement now keeps the tombstones it already holds (the bug is also in 3.2.3)
- Fixed: `sift_table`: a bitmap-scan `UPDATE` or `DELETE` that a `VACUUM` interrupted by promoting the write buffer silently skipped the rows of the promoted blocks (1530 of 3000 rows updated, no error); the scan now reads the write buffers it began with (the bug is also in 3.2.3)
- Fixed: pg_sift: a `GROUP BY` of an expression of a `date` column (`extract(dow FROM d)`, `d + 1`, `date_trunc('month', d)`) failed with `invalid memory alloc request size 17179869248` when the column held both `infinity` and `-infinity`, which are the two ends of the 32-bit range: the scan asked for a lookup table over all of it. Such a key now keeps its ids in a hash table, as a key of an integer column does, and the rows are those of the normal plan; the bug is also in 3.2.3, where the error reads `a column range of 4294967296 values is too wide for the aggregate scan`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a `GROUP BY` on a `date` column whose selected rows were all `infinity` (or all `-infinity`) stopped the backend: the range table of the group keys stepped over the end of the 32-bit range and wrote far outside the table (also in 3.2.3 and earlier; found while fixing the line above).
- Changed: pg_sift: the first row of each group asked with a window function (`WHERE rn = 1`, `rn <= 1` or `rn < 2` over a sub-select or a CTE read once, with the one window function `row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY .. ORDER BY ..)`) is also planned as `SELECT DISTINCT ON (..) .. ORDER BY ..`, and runs in the scan, where the order is a total one, instead of sorting every row through the window. A window it leaves as written (`rank()`, another window function, no `PARTITION BY` or `ORDER BY`, a sub-select that groups or limits) is named `window_first` by `sift.explain_why`.
- Changed: pg_sift: an aggregate of one table that a `LATERAL` sub-select or a scalar sub-select ties to the outer query by equalities (`(SELECT sum(f.a) FROM f WHERE f.k = o.k)`) is also planned as the table grouped by those columns and joined to the outer query, and that plan is kept when it runs in the scan and costs less than a lookup of the group for each outer row: a few outer rows keep the lookups. An outer row whose key has no row, or is NULL, gets 0 for a `count` and NULL for every other aggregate, as before; a sub-select that groups, limits, joins, reads the outer query outside the equalities or has an aggregate with a value over no rows stays as written.

## 3.2.3 - 2026-10-23

- Fixed: pg_sift: three reads and writes of memory the relation cache can free, found with `debug_discard_caches` and AddressSanitizer. A catalog read can reload the cached state of an index (`rd_amcache`) while an index function still holds it: the insert of a row that flags a leaf (a value the index cannot store, or a JSON null) wrote its write-buffer hint there after `sift_dir_update` (a segmentation fault in `col_index_crash`, or `could not open file ... target block`); the heap check of `sift.col_verify(index, true)` and the building of an index row of a table with a toasted value read the state after the detoast. Each now works on a copy or reads the fields it needs first. The effect is a backend crash (the server restarts every session) or corrupted heap memory in a narrow window, which a concurrent `VACUUM`, `ANALYZE` or `ALTER` of the table can open; no privilege is needed to be exposed to it. New tests: `col_insert_discard`, `col_row_relcache`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: the first statement of every new backend ran the ordinary plan: the library loads while the planner opens the first column index, after the planner hook had its chance, so the CTE-inline and lateral-flatten decisions were skipped (a statement of two arms over one CTE: 233 ms on the first statement of a backend, 3.7 ms on the second, rows equal). The executor start now plans a plain `SELECT` again through the hook, once per backend, when the hook never ran. Pools that recycle connections meet the case on every new backend; `session_preload_libraries = 'pg_sift'` avoided it. Not fixed in 3.2.3: a first statement with bound parameters (the extended protocol of a driver, `EXECUTE` with arguments, `pgbench -M extended` and `-M prepared`) still plans without the hook, once per backend (220 to 260 ms against 3 to 5 ms for the next statement); `session_preload_libraries = 'pg_sift'` or `shared_preload_libraries = 'pg_sift'` avoids it. New test: `agg_first_statement`.
- Fixed: macOS: `scripts/tap.sh`, and so every Perl TAP test, stopped on the BSD `sha256sum` (`-c --quiet` on standard input); `scripts/sha256.sh` runs GNU `sha256sum` or `shasum -a 256`, and `scripts/ci.sh --selftest` fails on a script or test that calls the system checksum tool directly. The SQLite build's portable-list filter reads `#` in a way GNU make 3.81, the stock macOS make, accepts (it stopped `make check` with "unterminated call to function filter-out").
- Fixed: `make check` on macOS. The stock bash 3.2 failed on the syntax of bash 4 and `make check` exited 0 with no test run; it now stops with `needs bash 4+ (brew install bash)`, a run that selects no job fails, and `scripts/cpu_time.py` and the job claim no longer need Linux (`prctl`, `flock`). `scripts/ci.sh --selftest` checks that a script with bash 4 constructs carries that first-line check.

## 3.2.2 - 2026-10-22

- The store size rows show the index or columnar data with the heap beside it; README.md drops the places table, the Vulkan table and the rows that are sums of others.
- Fixed: pg_sift: security: with row-level security on a table, the planner could estimate the rows of a partition or an inheritance child of it from the statistics of the `sift` index, which count the rows the policy hides, so the row counts of `EXPLAIN` (and the plan chosen) for a role under the policy could reveal information about the hidden rows (how many of them hold a term). The estimate is the planner's default now, as it already was for a table without partitions and as the documentation says ("no index estimate" under row-level security); the refusal of `sift.score()` on such a partition or child names the table under the policy (it said "needs a sift index on every partition"). No data was modified and no query result was affected: the rows a policy hides were never returned. Affected: 3.0.0 to 3.2.1 (3.2.0 and 3.2.1 tested, 3.0 and 3.1 by reading the code), a server where a role without `BYPASSRLS` that is not the owner reads, through its parent, a partitioned or inheritance table with a policy whose partitions or children carry a `sift` index. Nothing is needed before upgrading beyond installing the new library (no setting, no `REINDEX`, no change of the extension SQL). Test `rls_stats`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: the aggregate pushdown (`Custom Scan (SiftAgg)`) no longer refuses what the normal plan answers (3.2.0 and 3.2.1): a role with `SELECT` on the columns a query reads but not on the whole table, or on a view and not on its table, got `permission denied for table` at run time. The scan asked for table-level `SELECT` of the invoking role, where PostgreSQL has checked the columns, and the view owner's rights, before the scan starts; the scan's own check is gone (a partitioned table too), its row-level security check stays. A view over a table with row-level security keeps the normal plan.
- Known issue: pg_sift: in 3.2.0, 3.2.1 and 3.2.2 a role that is neither a superuser nor the owner of the extension cannot drop anything in a database where pg_sift is installed: the event trigger function `sift.table_paths_drop()` is not `SECURITY DEFINER`, so every `DROP` of that role (a table, index or schema, with or without pg_sift) fails with `permission denied for schema sift`. The extension SQL cannot change in a 3.2 patch release; `ALTER EXTENSION pg_sift UPDATE` to 3.3 fixes it. Until then, run once in each database with the extension, as a superuser (and again after restoring a dump, which creates the extension from the 3.2 script): `ALTER FUNCTION sift.table_paths_drop() SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp;` (`docs/UPGRADING.md`).

## 3.2.1 - 2026-10-22

- Added: bench: `bench/bench duckdb` measures pg_duckdb (the official release image, DuckDB reading the heap and a Parquet file it writes) against pg_sift and PostgreSQL on the store benchmark's orders and ten questions, in two containers with equal limits, warm and cold; the questions pg_duckdb cannot run are reported as not supported.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a set filter (`col = ANY($1)`, `col IN (..)`) of thousands of ids was slower in 3.2.0 than in 3.1.0 (3M facts, 100,000 ids: generic plan 10 to 125 ms, literal list 121 to 238 ms; 10,000 ids 5 to 15 ms). 3.2.0 sent the sorted members of every `IN` list with gaps along with its zone test, and the column scan rebuilt that test (copy, sort) at each range of units it began; a list of more than 256 members meets the zone of nearly every unit and is tested by its range again, as in 3.1 (a list of widely spread keys in the hash table keeps its members). Same data, host and run (7 runs, median): 100,000 ids generic plan / custom plan / literal list 9.8 / 110 / 123 ms, 10,000 ids 4.5 / 14 / 16 ms; `IN (SELECT ..)` of 100,000 members 141 to 27 ms. Test `agg_zone_or`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a column segment written under `sift.col_format_minor = 4` or 5 (the setting for readers of 3.1) carried the zone flag of a JSON null that only 3.2 knows, so `sift.col_verify` of pg_sift 3.1 reported such an index as corrupt (its answers were right); the flag is now written from minor 6 on. Test `col_zone_jnull`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL (.. LIMIT 1)` child read as a first-row lookup table (3.2.0) lost every group when the query summed or averaged a `float8` column and grouped by a column of the lookup row: the scan decided its lane order for float sums before the child became a table, so the groups were merged into a result that was never read, and the query returned no rows where the normal plan returned them. `count`, `min`, `max`, integer sums and a group key from the facts were not affected. Test `agg_first_row_lookup` (every aggregate kind grouped by the lookup row).
- Fixed: pg_sift: a session that read a new `sift_table` while its first writer was between writing the metapage and the tombstone segment cached the table as laid out; its own first write then added a write buffer to a directory that was never flagged as a `sift_table`, and every later read of the table failed with "The metapage does not carry the sift_table flag". The first write now checks that the segment exists, not only that the table was read.
- Fixed: pg_sift: an `ORDER BY .. LIMIT` read from the column units no longer fails with `sift: top-N row (p,o) is not in the heap` after an `UPDATE` that leaves every indexed value as it was (a HOT update): the index entry names the root of the HOT chain, and the scan now reads the row as an index scan does, through the chain. Test `col_topn_hot`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: on PostgreSQL 18, two `EXISTS` (or `IN`) over the same table by its unique key with different conditions (`EXISTS (.. i.id = e.parent_id AND i.kind = 'k1') AND EXISTS (.. i.id = e.parent_id AND i.kind = 'k4')`) are merged by the planner into one scan of the table (self-join elimination) and `SiftAgg` applied the conditions of one of them: a count of 1,200 where the rows are 0. A semi, anti or preserved join whose table the planner removed this way keeps the normal plan. Test `agg_semi_same_table`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a row inserted into a `sift_table` could be lost with its insert reported done. The writer that closes a write buffer swaps it in the directory and flags its pages sealed afterwards; a session that held a page of the closed buffer kept appending to it until then, and the promotion (VACUUM, `sift.table_maintain`, a worker) that ran in between released the page with the row on it. The promotion now flags every page sealed before it reads the buffer, as the promotion of a BM25 or column write buffer does. Test `009_tbl_concurrent` (`sift.table_seal_gate` holds the closing writer before it flags). No format change; present in 3.2.0.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a parallel worker of a `SiftAgg` scan over a partitioned table crashed when an item of its work lay in a partition other than the first: the leader hands the workers the superblock offsets of every partition, but a worker opened the first partition only, so the item ran on a source that was not there. A worker now opens the partition it moves to. Test `agg_par_partitions`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: every query on a table with a `sift` index scanned `pg_opclass` several times while it was planned (3.2.0): the check of a `sift.score(ctid) > c` cutoff read the operator class of `float8` before it looked at the operands of each clause. A short ranked query cost 6% to 23% more user instructions than in 3.1.0 (20,000 rows in one segment: `and` 1.23x, `phrase` 1.19x, `count` 1.14x, `rank` 1.11x, `or` 1.07x; 61 segments 1.01x to 1.03x), the loss of queries per second the 3.2.0 benchmark saw on `phrase` and `and`; now 1.00x to 1.02x (`bench/steps/cpu_gate.sh`). Test `rank_score_filter`.

## 3.2.0 - 2026-10-22

- Changed: pg_sift: a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL (SELECT .. FROM t WHERE t.a = x AND t.b = y LIMIT 1)` over a table with a unique index on the columns it compares is read by SiftAgg as a lookup table (its rows hashed by key, kept for the next query like any other lookup table) instead of running the child for every distinct input: the key has one row, so the row is the one any plan returns. The planner prices both and takes the cheaper; a key the table repeats, a condition, an order, an offset or an expression over the parameter keep the child (`EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows `First Row Lookup Tables`). Three keys over a 100,000-row table and 60,000 facts: 47 ms (269 ms in the normal plan, 167 ms in a scan that ran the child).
- Changed: pg_sift: the arms of a `UNION ALL` that join the same lookup table and read the same rows run in one pass over the index also when they ask for other aggregates: the pass computes the union of them and each arm's set updates its own, the lookup being resolved once (three arms over a lookup join, 100,000 documents: 18.7 ms scanned arm by arm, 8.6 ms in one pass; `sift.agg_fuse = off` runs the arms alone).
- Changed: pg_sift: `sift_table` reads the directory again only when its version changed, where every row operation re-read it (and asked the file system for its length): `UPDATE` of 10% of 200,000 rows takes 171 ms instead of 134, `DELETE` 93 ms instead of 85 (median of 5, one client, 2026-10-21, PostgreSQL 18.6; the host was busy). No format change.
- Changed: pg_sift: appending a record to a write buffer page (`sift_col`, `sift` and `sift_table`) and a tombstone (`DELETE`, `UPDATE` of a `sift_table`) log the changed bytes of the page directly instead of copying the page, comparing it and copying it back for the generic WAL record, which is the same record and is replayed as before: `INSERT .. SELECT` of 200,000 rows into a `sift_table` takes 320 ms instead of 366, a loop of 100,000 single-row `INSERT`s 312 ms instead of 354, `DELETE` of 10% of the rows 14% faster, and 1 to 4% less into `sift_col` and `sift` indexes (median of 5, one client, 2026-10-21, PostgreSQL 18.6; the host was busy). No format change.
- Changed: pg_sift: the arms of a `UNION ALL` that read the same rows for the same aggregates and join the same lookup table run in one pass over the index also when their `WHERE` differs: the conjuncts every arm has are the pass's, what an arm has beyond them is tested on the rows of its own set, and the units and rows no arm needs are not read (a pass whose arms the pre-aggregates answer one by one is not made). `sift.agg_fuse = off` runs each arm alone.
- Changed: pg_sift: a row of a unit that holds a value a path cannot store is read again from the heap only if the conditions the scan can test on the stored unit (a key set, a comparison or NOT NULL on a stored column) let it through, and not at all when they reject it: a query over 200,000 rows with 4.3% such rows, a scope of 51 keys and a rare NOT NULL column reads 7 rows again, not 8,696 (8.5 to 2.2 ms, one run each). The rows and errors are those of the normal plan; no format change.
- Changed: pg_sift: the normal plan of a lookup join PostgreSQL can count (`IN (SELECT ..)`, a join on a list table) is priced for the rows the column's most common values own when the column is more than twice as uneven as a flat one, not for the mean rows of a key, so `Custom Scan (SiftAgg)` is chosen for a short list of heavy parents (5 parents holding a third of 200k rows: 34 ms against 8.6 ms); a rare list takes the same plan, 5 to 8 ms slower than the normal one at 200k rows (`sift.agg_key_skew = off` gives the mean back).
- Changed: pg_sift: the scan of `SiftAgg` is priced at the speed it runs: its own operators (reads, filters, tuples, keys, aggregate updates, the merge of workers) at 0.2 of the count the cost model gave them (`AGG_SCAN_SPEED`; the shapes it runs alone take 0.4 to 0.9 ns for an operator counted at 3 ns), the arms of a `UNION ALL` under `SiftAggRows` that run as one pass pay the first arm's price and a tenth of the others, the start-up is 0.6 ms and the worker start 22 times `parallel_setup_cost` (was 110, scaled with the scan). Shapes that were 8 to 35 times faster as a scan but lost the cost comparison (a free-key member by month, periods from a `VALUES` list, a count of an unnest) now take it; a few rows of one parent keep the btree plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: the normal plan of a query whose scope is a lookup PostgreSQL cannot count (`IN (SELECT ..)` or a join on a table filtered by a jsonb path or an expression, a sub-select, a CTE) is priced for the keys the scanned column can hold instead of its one-row guess, so SiftAgg is chosen when the list is long; a lookup whose filters have statistics, a VALUES list and a list of constants keep their estimate (`sift.agg_param_set_size = 0` turns it off).
- Added: pg_sift: a `sift_table` is defragmented without `VACUUM` (`sift.table_auto_defrag`, off by default in 3.2: an opt-in until it has run on real workloads, `sift.table_auto_defrag_free_pct`, `sift.table_auto_defrag_delay`, `sift.table_auto_defrag_indexes`): the backend that closes a write buffer asks a background worker for a pass when the tombstones reach `sift.table_dead_rewrite_pct` of the rows (a whole table VACUUM, index cleanup included, throttled by the cost delay) or the free blocks of the file reach a quarter of it (`sift.table_maintain`); the worker stays a few seconds and passes again while the writer's rows settle, and returns the free end of the file with the conditional locks of `VACUUM`. Test `017_tbl_autodefrag`.
- Added: pg_sift: `sift.table_maintain(tbl)` does the work of `VACUUM` on a `sift_table`'s column segments and file without its index cleanup: it promotes the write buffers, clears the dead rows older than every snapshot, rewrites the mostly dead segments (`sift.table_dead_rewrite_pct`), gathers the free space and returns the free end of the file, taking the table and the end-of-file lock only when they are free. The tombstone log and the index entries stay for the next `VACUUM`, which still removes them (test `tbl_maintain`, isolation `tbl_maintain`).
- Added: pg_sift: a `sift_col` index that churn scattered is rewritten by a background worker after the `VACUUM` that finds it so (`sift.col_auto_merge`, off by default): the worker seals the write buffer and merges every segment into one, as `sift.col_promote` plus `sift.col_merge(index, true)` do, under the maintenance lock only (readers and writers are not blocked; skipped when the lock is held, never on a standby), sleeping as `sift.maintenance_cost_delay` says and rewriting an index over `sift.vacuum_rewrite_limit` MB by partitions of that size. The rule: more than `sift.col_auto_merge_dirty_pct` (30) percent of the units hold a dead row, or the units listed are `sift.col_auto_merge_fragmentation` (2.0) times the superblocks they cover, or the write buffer holds more than `sift.col_auto_merge_buffer_pct` (1.0) percent of the rows. A 1% DELETE/UPDATE spread over the table used to leave a dead row in every unit and no unit answering from its pre-aggregates, with `dead_rewrite_pct` never reached. `sift.col_maintain(index)` applies the rule in a session (a `pg_cron` job) and `sift.col_maintenance_due(index)` returns the reason. The merges of the maintenance worker of a `sift_col` index now honour `sift.maintenance_cost_delay`, which only the `sift` index's did. No format change. Tests `col_auto_merge` (regress, isolation, TAP).
- Changed: pg_sift: `sift.col_merge(index, true)` seals the open write buffer before it merges, so one call is `col_promote` plus the merge: the rows written since the last promotion go into the one segment it makes. Before, they stayed in the buffer as one in-memory unit per superblock they touched, next to the merged segment (no pre-aggregates, one extra unit each). Without `force` nothing changes. `sift.merge(index, true)` of a `sift` index keeps leaving the buffer open. No format change. Test `col_merge_force`.
- Changed: pg_sift: the planner prices a top-N scan that walks its sort column by the zones (`sift.topn_walk`) with the units the zones leave, from the mean zone width the index stores plus `n` over the expected matches, instead of all of them. 396,000 rows, `ORDER BY key, id LIMIT 10` with the scan forced: the estimated cost of a key in blocks of 12,000, 3,000 and 300 rows falls from 5,143 to 757, 655 and 1,446; a random key stays at 5,142.
- Changed: pg_sift: `sift.table_dead_rewrite_pct` defaults to 30 (was 50): `VACUUM` rewrites a `sift_table` column segment once a third of its rows is dead instead of half, so a table with scattered deletes and updates keeps a file near 1.4 times its live rows instead of up to 2 times (the rewrite costs `VACUUM` about 15-20% more time in a steady delete/update/insert workload). No format change; `SET sift.table_dead_rewrite_pct = 50` restores the old behaviour.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a conjunct on the member of one key of a map leaf (`CAST(jsonb_extract_path(doc, 'k') AS integer) = 8`) next to a `jsonb_each` cast of the members keeps the normal plan when some unit holds a value the leaf cannot store: the scan dropped the row of a dirty member (the filter saw NULL) where the normal plan raises the cast error, with the message of the conjunct's own type. A query without such a conjunct is unchanged.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg no longer raises "holds values its index cannot store" where the normal plan answers: a filter, key or aggregate on the member of one key of a map leaf next to a `jsonb_each` cast of the members (a dirty value of another key, in a row the filter drops), the string `-0` in a float column of `jsonb_to_recordset`, and a list of more than 2^20 elements are read again from the heap like the other dirty rows (the string `-0` counts as 0). Index builds over such documents already flag the value and never fail.
- Changed: pg_sift: the rows of a joined table are kept whatever the size of its heap: the memory of the rows (half of `sift.agg_lookup_cache_kb` at most) decides, not a limit of 32,768 pages. Reading a table's pages to check it has not changed costs about 0.4 microseconds a page, a twentieth of loading its rows (25,000 rows: 3.3 ms to load, 0.09 ms to check; 200,000 rows: 27 ms and 0.47 ms). A table whose rows did not fit is remembered, and not read page by page on every query.
- Changed: pg_sift: the arms of a statement that read different columns of the same joined table share one kept entry of its rows (the union of the columns), instead of one entry per arm: two arms over a 500,000-row mapping table keep 13.6 MB, not 23.3 MB.
- Changed: pg_sift: a lookup join keyed by a text column translates each value of the leaf to the table's key value once per scan, not once per combination of values it is in.
- Changed: pg_sift: the rows a backend keeps of a joined table (`sift.agg_lookup_cache_kb`) take a sixth of the memory: a key or a column value that repeats is stored once and a row holds its id (a 500,000-row mapping table keyed by three text columns: 76 MB, now 12 MB per kept table), and a probe finds the ids of a key's values before it walks the rows. Tests `agg_lookup_rows` and the text key permutation of the isolation spec `agg_lookup_cache`.
- Changed: pg_sift: a lookup join probe allocates the rows its key matches instead of one slot per row of the table (a 500,000-row mapping table: 2 MB for every combination of values): 200,000 combinations of three keys over 260,000 tuples, one backend, 571 to 427 ms with the table cached, 757 to 534 ms loaded for the query.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg counts `count(DISTINCT x)` of a column the per-row aggregates group by (a parent id under an outer aggregate over `GROUP BY id, parent_id, ..` arms) in the scan, as the distinct ids of that key per outer group (a bitmap, then a hash set, then a sort, within `hash_mem`), instead of leaving the plan to PostgreSQL's sort of every per-row group. Test `agg_arm_distinct`.
- Changed: pg_sift: the cells of per-row aggregates that group by more than the outer keys (a parent id next to the id, an element's tag) are numbered inside their row and no longer in the group table, which then holds the outer groups only: the table that grew with the data and the per-cell arrays that went with it are gone, and the results, their order and the stored data are unchanged. Two per-row arms over 200,000 rows with 442,754 elements and a `count(DISTINCT parent_id)`: 549 ms to 156 ms (median of 3, interleaved, busy host); 25,000 rows: 67 to 20 ms. Test `agg_arm_cells`.
- Changed: pg_sift: the per-row aggregates of an outer aggregate (UNION ALL arms grouped by a row id) may read a joined VALUES list, sub-select or `LIMIT 1` lookup: the outer aggregate runs in the one scan with the joins as lookups. The plans of the joined rows are sub-plans of the arms' query level (planned against its own `PlannerInfo`, not the statement's); `sift.agg_two_level = off` keeps the arms' own scans.
- Changed: pg_sift: a document that a `sift_col` list leaf cannot store (a document that is an array, a list that is a string) no longer keeps the normal plan for an `EXISTS` over the list with a lookup per element (the joined mapping of a report by label) or for an integer cast of a member next to an unnest of the list: the rows are read again from the heap and the error of a list that SQL cannot read is raised for the rows that reach it, after the join of the test or the cast, as the normal plan does.
- Changed: pg_sift: a dirty value no longer keeps the normal plan for the two reads that are exact per member: the member of one key (`LEFT JOIN LATERAL jsonb_each(doc) r ON r.key = 'k'` with `CAST(r.value AS float8 | integer | bigint)`) is read again from the heap only for the rows whose member of that key holds a value that is no number, and raises the error of the cast for them (message and SQLSTATE) while a dirty member of another key raises nothing; and an `EXISTS` over the elements of a `jsonb_to_recordset` list passes a row whose list raises, which then raises where the rows reach the aggregates, as the normal plan does when it evaluates the test. A query that mixes two casts of the member, filters on it, casts it in a branch, or tests the list under `OR` keeps the normal plan. No format change.
- Changed: pg_sift: an outer group key that is NULL in every arm of a two level aggregate (a column of a generated series, a bucket the arms do not break down) no longer keeps the normal plan: the key is NULL in every group of the scan.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a `SiftAgg` combo (a lookup join keyed by dictionary values, an expression over them) finds the entry of a combination of values by a hash that spreads small ids: with hundreds of thousands of distinct combinations the table of entries used to pile them in a few slots and walk thousands of entries per tuple (200,000 combinations of three keys over 260,000 tuples, a 500,000-row mapping table, one backend: 37.3 s, now 0.57 s). Test `agg_combo_hash`.
- Changed: pg_sift: a top-N scan (`ORDER BY key LIMIT n`) without a search over a `sift_col` key that does not follow the table (values in blocks in no order, random) reads the zones of all the superblocks first, visits the superblocks by the best row their zone can hold and ends at the first that cannot beat the n-th row (`sift.topn_walk`, default on; `off` visits them in table order). The rows are the same.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg reads again from the heap the rows whose document a text path of a `sift_col` index cannot store (`doc ->> 'k'`, an `astext` leaf: a container where the path is a scalar, a document that is an array) as `->>` reads them, instead of keeping the normal plan for every query that reads the path.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a `jsonb_typeof` test of a path that the query also reads (`doc ->> 'k'`) no longer makes the aggregate scan raise `0A000` where the normal plan runs: a test that keeps a type the path cannot store (an object, an array, no value: the paths of a document that is an array) keeps the normal plan once an index unit holds such a value, one that keeps only the types the path stores (`string`, `number`, `boolean` of a text path) is still answered by the scan.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg reads a partitioned table also when a parameter of a generic plan or a stable function (`d >= $1`, `k = ANY ($1)`, `ts >= now() - interval '30 days'`) prunes its partitions when the plan starts: the scan evaluates the operand when it starts and does not read the partitions whose bounds it refutes (`Partitions Removed` in `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`; parallel workers take the leader's superblocks). Before, a parameter on the partition key kept the normal plan, and a stable bound read every partition. A table split by hash prunes the same way: the scan computes the remainder of each value an equality, `= ANY ($1)` or a stable function leaves the key (the key's hash function, the modulus and remainder of each partition, every column of a multi-column key) and does not read the partitions that cannot hold it; another test of the hash column (`k > $1`), another type of the operand and an expression key keep the normal plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: the keys of an inner or semi join (the join key filter) drop the partitions of a partitioned table that none of them can be in when the join column is a column the table is split by: a range or list bound is refuted by the range of the keys and, with few keys, by each of them, a hash level by the remainders of the keys, and no key reads no partition (`Partitions Removed`). Before, the scan read every partition and the zones of the units pruned the units of range and list partitions only.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg reads a partitioned table of up to 4,096 partitions (was 1,024), so a stable bound or a parameter that prunes a table of thousands of partitions to a few keeps the scan: the scan opens only the partitions pruning leaves (it opened all of them: 3,000 partitions pruned to one, 92 ms to 9.5 ms of execution) and releases the buffers a partition keeps pinned when it moves to the next (a scan of 7,000 partitions failed with `no unpinned buffers available` at `shared_buffers = 64MB`; it now keeps those of one partition pinned). A parallel worker opens only the partitions the leader reads. Test `agg_partitions_many`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a `sift_col` build (`CREATE INDEX`, `REINDEX`, in a parallel worker too) and the aggregate scan's read of rows again from the heap no longer crash the backend when the index's relcache entry is reloaded meanwhile: they kept the index's cached state across the tuples, which that reload frees. The build now keeps a copy of its own. Test `col_index_relcache` (`sift.test_col_rel_inval`, testing builds).
- Added: pg_sift: a backend keeps the rows of the tables a `SiftAgg` scan joined (the lookup tables of `JOIN`, `IN (SELECT ..)`) for its next query while the table is unchanged and vacuumed (`sift.agg_lookup_cache_kb`, 256 MB per backend, 0 disables; `Cached Lookup Tables` in `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`): a mapping table of 500,000 rows is no longer loaded again for every query. A write, a rollback, a vacuum, a rewrite or a table with pages not yet visible to all transactions loads the rows again.
- Added: pg_sift: `Custom Scan (SiftAggRows)` groups the rows of a plan that reads SiftAgg nodes when an aggregate counts distinct integers (the outer `count(DISTINCT id)` of a report over per-row aggregates, joined to a spine of periods): the counts, `sum` and `avg` are kept per group and each `count(DISTINCT x)` is a set of (group, value) pairs, which moves to a sort of narrow tuples when it outgrows `hash_mem`, instead of PostgreSQL's sort of every wide row and a sorted aggregate per `DISTINCT`. `sift.agg_rows` turns it off. `avg` and `sum` of floats add in the order the rows arrive, as the scan's own aggregates do.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg reads a partial `sift_col` index (`CREATE INDEX .. WHERE pred`) when the conditions of the query imply its predicate, instead of ignoring it; the index is smaller and a query that does not imply the predicate keeps the normal plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg prices the rows it reads again from the heap (values a `sift_col` leaf cannot store, such as a numeric string in a number path) by the share of units that hold one instead of a fixed share of the table, and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows `Units Read Again From The Heap` next to `Rows Read Again From The Heap`.
- Fixed: pg_sift: a table with several `sift_col` indexes is scanned through the first of them that gives a SiftAgg plan. SiftAgg took only the index PostgreSQL lists first (the newest), so a second index with other columns or paths left the queries only the older one can answer on the normal plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: a backend keeps the parsed dictionaries of the segments of a `sift_col` index (up to 64 MB, least recently used first) for its next query: a scan that reads a string leaf again no longer inflates and indexes the leaf's dictionary (a free text path of 60,000 entries took about 1.3 ms per segment on every query; the second query of a session takes none, with identical answers). A rewritten segment has its own. No format change.
- Fixed: pg_sift: `sift.col_verify(index, true)` no longer crashes the backend when another session's `VACUUM` or `ANALYZE` updates the index's statistics while it checks the heap: the check kept the index's cached state, which that update frees, across the tuples. Test `verify_relcache` (`sift.test_col_verify_inval`, testing builds).
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg prices a scan filtered by a lookup join by the units the zones of its keys leave (the mean zone width of the leaf, a key a point of the range), and a key filter of widely spread keys skips the units whose zone holds none of them
- Changed: a scan that the cost of the rollups chose for three units in a row (their tuples cost at least a quarter more than their elements) skips the exactness check and the rollup read of the next sixteen units, so a query on an index whose rollups do not pay is not slower than with `sift.agg_preagg = off`.
- Added: the rollups of the elements of a list answer `GROUPING SETS`, `ROLLUP` and `CUBE` over the dims (each set folds the tuples again under its own columns, the keys outside it NULL), also with `FILTER`, tests on dims and derived keys; `GROUPING()` and the sets over the rows keep the scan.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg runs an `EXISTS` over the elements of an array whose test reads a lookup per element (`LEFT JOIN LATERAL (SELECT .. FROM mapping WHERE mapping.k = elem.k AND mapping.kind = dim.kind LIMIT 1)`) or a column of a joined table (the label of an event by the kind of its parent): the lookup runs once for each distinct value of the columns it reads, a row passes when some element does, and a row whose elements and joined value repeat is not tested again. `NOT EXISTS`, `EXISTS` in an aggregate `FILTER` and expressions that can raise keep the normal plan.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg tests the keys of its inner and semi lookup joins before tuples are made also in a plan with a preserved side (a date spine `LEFT JOIN` rows that join tables of their own), keeps only the keys that the joins keyed by a column of the table can match (`JOIN ids ON ids.id = d.id` after `JOIN d ON d.id = f.d_id`), and reads no unit when a join has no key at all; a dictionary entry gets its id in a join when a tuple first reads it, not all of them when the scan meets the dictionary (a column of millions of free texts).
- Changed: pg_sift: the arms of a `UNION ALL` that join the same common table expression (a `VALUES` list of periods, a list of ids read by every arm) run in one pass over the index, whatever columns of it each arm reads: the list is loaded once, the scan, the lookups and the rows read again from the heap are shared. Another list, or another condition on it in one arm, keeps its own pass. A sub-select or function joined as a plan still keeps one pass per arm.
- Changed: pg_sift: the arms of a `UNION ALL` that look a name up with the same correlated `LIMIT 1` sub-select (a mapping table joined on values of the unnested list) run in one pass over the index although they read other columns of it or ask for other expressions of it (one the mapped name, one also its sub-name): the scan, the lookups and the rows read again from the heap are shared. An arm with an inner join (the `IS NOT NULL` of a mapped name) and an arm with a left join of the same sub-select (all the lines) run in the same pass too: the pass joins left and the arm with the inner join drops the lines without a row; an arm with another condition keeps its own pass.
- Changed: pg_sift: the arms of a `UNION ALL` that read the same rows for the same aggregates run in one pass over the index also when they join a table (a lookup join or a combo the arms share), read leaves of their own, or have more than four keys together (a key two arms share counts once; up to 31 distinct keys): the rows read again from the heap for values the index cannot store are read once for all the arms, and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows their count on every arm.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg evaluates `jsonb_exists(doc, 'k')` of the document whose list (`jsonb_to_recordset(doc->'items')`, `jsonb_array_elements(doc->'items')`) it unnests, in keys, `CASE` branches and filters, from the JSON type of the member `k` the index holds (a JSON `null` member exists); it stays with PostgreSQL elsewhere, where an array or a string document can hold the key.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg reads again from the heap the rows of `jsonb_to_recordset` whose document holds a value the index cannot store (a number written as a string) also when the query reads the column inside an expression (a `CASE` key, a bucket of the value): the query kept PostgreSQL's plan before.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg joins a sub-select whose plan PostgreSQL gave a condition pushed down into it (the run condition of a ranking window: `IN (SELECT .. FROM (SELECT .., row_number() OVER (..) rn ..) r WHERE rn <= 3)`) instead of keeping the normal plan: the plan of the sub-select enforces the condition and does not output its column, and the scan no longer tests it again.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg runs aggregates whose derived table has a `WITH` of its own (a ranked top-N over an aggregate, `UNION ALL` arms over one CTE): the CTEs of a derived table that reads nothing around it are lookup plans of the scan, and a CTE of it that several aggregates read is planned inline like the statement's own.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg runs an `EXISTS` over the elements of an array that is written with a sub-select PostgreSQL pulls up, and the conditions of the nullable input of an outer join (a date spine `LEFT JOIN` a sub-select or CTE) that test its arrays (an `EXISTS`, `jsonb_path_exists`) and the aggregate filters that read a test computed there: the `ON` conjuncts that read only the scanned table filter its rows before matching, and a test guarded by the absent input is read through the guard when it is not true for an absent input.
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg prices the filters and expressions on the values of an object member or array element once per distinct value (PostgreSQL's default number of distinct values, not once per row), so a per-key metric aggregate with `FILTER (WHERE value >= 8)` is chosen when the scan is cheaper.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg no longer fills a table of the whole range of an integer group key (a site id of a table ordered by another column, whose units hold few values far apart) before it uses the hash table: such a key now costs the groups it has, not its range, which made a per-key aggregate several times slower than PostgreSQL.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg aggregates `GROUPING SETS` / `ROLLUP` over group keys computed inside the nullable input of an outer join (a date spine `LEFT JOIN` a CTE or sub-select that derives the keys) on PostgreSQL 18: the keys are the expressions of the input, NULL for the rows no input row matched, instead of the query being left to PostgreSQL.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg counted the preserved row of an outer join that no row matched as one value of `count(DISTINCT key)` when the key is the unique column of the scanned table (a date spine `LEFT JOIN` an empty selection answered 1 where PostgreSQL answers 0).
- Changed: pg_sift: SiftAgg prices a lookup join without keys (a date spine joined on an expression) by the distinct values the scan's own conditions leave and by the rows a set-returning spine makes, not by every distinct value of the table and PostgreSQL's 1000 rows a call. Aggregates over a date spine and correlated arrays of a joined row pick the scan on their own costs where it is faster.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg no longer reads a value the index cannot store (a string, an object or an array in a `float8` path, a scalar where an array is declared) as NULL when the query takes a key of an object leaf with free keys (`(metrics ->> 'k')::float8`, `(metrics -> 'k')::float8`) or `jsonb_array_length(doc -> 'l')`: such a query keeps the normal plan, so a numeric string converts and anything else raises PostgreSQL's own error, and a plan made before the value was written raises the scan's error. In the columns of `jsonb_to_recordset` a string `NaN` or `Infinity` converts instead of raising the scan's overflow error (the string `-0` still raises it).
- Added: pg_sift: an expression key or predicate that reads one column twice under binary-compatible types (the column, and the column relabeled for a comparison with a `char(n)` column of an `IN (SELECT ..)`) runs in SiftAgg.
- Added: pg_sift: a correlated child of SiftAgg (a `UNION` arm, a lateral sub-select) can read the value of a `jsonb_each` member and cast it: the child is given the jsonb the stored number stands for, runs only for the members a row reaches, and raises PostgreSQL's own cast error. `x IS TRUE` over an existence test (`jsonb_path_exists`, `@?`) runs in the scan.
- Added: pg_sift: a lateral sub-select that PostgreSQL leaves in place because its query is read on the nullable side of an outer join (`dates LEFT JOIN cte`) is pulled up inside its own query level when that gives a SiftAgg plan that costs less, so `jsonb_each(doc)` joined to a mapping table runs in the scan instead of as a correlated child of the whole document.
- Added: pg_sift: `LEFT JOIN LATERAL jsonb_each(doc) r ON r.key = 'k'` (a constant key) runs in SiftAgg on PostgreSQL 18 as the member `k` of each row, with its NULL columns for the rows without it and the casts of its value (also inside a correlated child) raising PostgreSQL's own errors.
- Fixed: pg_sift: SiftAgg no longer treats `LEFT JOIN LATERAL jsonb_each(..)` / `jsonb_array_elements(..)` as an inner join: the rows without a member (NULL columns) were dropped and the ON clause was ignored. Such queries keep the normal plan.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg joins a lateral sub-select, a `UNION` / `UNION ALL` of arms, `generate_series`, `unnest` of an array column or a set-returning function that reads the joined row. PostgreSQL's parameterized plan of it is the child, run once per distinct input, with duplicates and NULL-extended rows as in the normal plan. A call of a document column that cannot raise (`jsonb_exists` or `?`, `->>`, `jsonb_typeof`) takes the index expression column of that call as the parameter. A child that can raise (a division, a cast of text, a sub-select) is joined only when the query joins nothing else, and one that reads a column of a query above the aggregate's own never is, so no statement fails only under the scan. A `UNION ALL` / `UNION` of sub-selects, a sub-select of a statement-level CTE and a `WITH` inside the sub-select are lookup plans too.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg reads the columns of `jsonb_to_recordset` from an index that flags values its typed leaves cannot store (a numeric string in a number path, a document that is an array or a scalar, a number in a text path). The flagged rows are read again from the heap and read as the function reads them, so the answer, errors included, is the normal plan's; `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows `Rows Read Again From The Heap`. No format change.
- Added: pg_sift: an inner or semi lookup join on an integer or date leaf tests the leaf against the keys of the loaded table before tuples are made (`sift.agg_join_filter`, `Join Key Filters` in `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`): units the keys' range misses are not read and the other rows are tested as a set instead of through the combo cache.
- Changed: pg_sift: a combo of one small key finds its cached entry by array index instead of a hash probe and `memcmp`.
- Added: pg_sift: `UNION ALL` arms of one statement that join the same table with the same columns and conditions load it once (`sift.agg_share_lookups`, `Shared Lookup Tables`); nothing is kept between statements.
- Added: pg_sift: hashed `IN (SELECT ..)` / `NOT IN (SELECT ..)` tests inside group keys, predicates and aggregates of an outer join's nullable input, row-valued tests and tests of an `int8` column run in SiftAgg (the scan asks the sub-plan for each tuple that reaches it).
- Fixed: pg_sift: grouping by more than eight keys with the two-level aggregate no longer overflows its key mask.
- Fixed: aggregate null-input markers cannot alias PostgreSQL executor parameters; preserved-input expressions can be explained on PostgreSQL 17. Predicate-only lookups account for testing every candidate row.
- Added: grouping expressions and aggregate FILTER predicates can read up to eight scalar inputs. Expression caches store and compare every input, with storage sized to the actual tuple width.
- Added: aggregate FILTER predicates over lookup columns preserve duplicate matches and NULL extensions, and evaluate expressions only after all joins select their input.
- Fixed: aggregate planning ignores unique LEFT joins PostgreSQL has removed, avoiding a planner crash. Preserved buckets can contain several dependent lookups; empty buckets retain NULL values for inner expressions and constants.
- Fixed: VACUUM relocation invalidates cached write targets when each replacement is published, including when maintenance later errors before truncation; failed unpublished copies return their allocations and follow maintenance backoff.
- Added: Homebrew formula in the source repository for PostgreSQL 17 and 18, tested on macOS arm64 with BM25, extension upgrade and column aggregate smoke tests; bitmap dispatch uses the existing scalar kernels on non-x86 CPUs.
- Added: pg_sift: `col @@> ANY (array)` is scanned as the OR of its elements, scored and ranked like `col @@> 'a OR b'` (`sift.score()`, `ORDER BY sift.score(...)`, `LIMIT`, `count(*)` and the filters work with it); a NULL element is skipped and a NULL or empty array matches no rows. Measured against the bitmap scan it replaces: `count(*)` 1.6 to 54 times faster, `LIMIT 10` and `LIMIT 1000` 1.5 to 130 times faster (0.1 ms slower on queries that take under 0.2 ms), the full match list equal or up to 2 times faster, planning under 0.6 ms at 100 elements. A list with no score or order that is estimated above 100,000 rows (after a `LIMIT`) keeps the bitmap scan, which was as fast or faster there.
- Changed: column format minor 6 guarantees complete JSON kinds for declared paths, including element leaves. This fixes wrong answers after merging a minor-3 segment into minor 4: JSON null could become a missing key. Old minors 3 through 5 and legacy write buffers remain readable; queries needing kinds those inputs cannot prove keep the normal plan, including partition parents. REINDEX and a sift_table VACUUM FULL reconstruct the kinds from the documents. No extension SQL changes are needed.
- Compatibility: a 3.1 library refuses a minor-6 column segment with a format error and REINDEX hint. Install 3.2 on every reader before writing minor 6. `sift.col_format_minor = 4` keeps new narrow segments readable by 3.1; 3 keeps the 3.0 layout, while wide leaves still require at least minor 5. Schemas without JSON paths keep their previous minor.
- Fixed: VACUUM returns internal free space of `sift_col`, `sift_table` and `sift` to the operating system by moving tail segments into existing free runs before truncating. Relocation preserves the stored format, reserves every destination below the planned end, and shares one write allowance across all moves. `vacuum_truncate = false` disables it; a conflicting reader skips it without waiting.
- Changed: `sift_col` VACUUM promotes sufficiently filled open write buffers and continues merging within `sift.vacuum_rewrite_limit` (default 128 MB). The same setting separately caps page relocation; zero retains the first column merge and ordinary free-tail truncation while disabling relocation. No on-disk format change.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg answers DISTINCT ON over indexed columns. The first row of each group by the ORDER BY is kept while the rows pass, with no sort, in the leader and the workers, when the ORDER BY holds the columns of a unique `NOT NULL` index; a tie-break that is not unique, a volatile or array value and groups that do not fit the hash memory keep the normal plan (`docs/AGG.md`). Test `agg_distinct_on`; fixtures `do_*`.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg aggregates over the preserved side of outer joins. `RIGHT JOIN`, `FULL JOIN` and a `LEFT JOIN` whose right side is the indexed table keep the rows of the other table that nothing matched, as one row of NULLs in the groups; a scan with such a join runs without workers.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg pushes down ctid range predicates. `ctid` `=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` and `BETWEEN` against a constant TID, at the top level of the `WHERE`, read only the superblocks of the range and test the rows of its first and last block (HOT chains included); `<>`, expressions, `OR`, lists, partitioned tables and top-N keep the normal plan (`docs/AGG.md`).
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg on partitioned tables whose partitions carry the same sift_col index. The partitions left after pruning are read as one source (each through its own index, whatever its attribute numbers, with parallel workers over all of them) and `EXPLAIN` lists them; a partition without the index or with other options, an inheritance parent, `ONLY`, and a parameter that prunes at run time keep the normal plan. Test `agg_partitions`; fixtures `pa_*`.
- Added: pg_sift: SiftAgg pushes down `NOT IN (SELECT ...)` and uncorrelated `IN` sub-selects under `OR`. PostgreSQL's hashed `SubPlan` runs once as the lookup table of the column, so the NULL rule is the normal plan's; a correlated, volatile or raising sub-select keeps the normal plan, and the scan runs without workers. Test `agg_subplan_lut` and the `t_not_in_subquery*` coldiff fixtures.
- Added: pg_sift: sift_col indexes inet, cidr, macaddr, macaddr8, timetz, bit and varbit columns.
